<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every hyperbolic group whose boundary is homeomorphic to the two-sphere admits a proper cocompact isometric action on hyperbolic three-space with finite kernel. This resolves Cannon's conjecture positively.

- **claim-cannon-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $G$ be a hyperbolic group with $\partial G$ homeomorphic to $\mathbb S^2$. There is a homomorphism $\rho:G\to\mathop{\mathrm{Isom}}(\mathbb H^3)$ for which the action on $\mathbb H^3$ is proper and cocompact. Its kernel is finite.
  - Explanation: Every hyperbolic group whose boundary is a topological two-sphere acts properly and cocompactly by isometries on hyperbolic three-space, with finite kernel.
- **claim-expansion-formula** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-expansion)): Let $v$ be the depth-$k$ vertex on a based ray to $x\in Z$, put $h=v^{-1}$ and $R=r_k$, and set $D_y=\max\{R,d(x,y)\}$. There are constants $c_E,C_E>0$, independent of these choices, such that $$c_E\frac{R\,d(y,z)}{D_yD_z} \le d(hy,hz)\le C_E\frac{R\,d(y,z)}{D_yD_z}. \label{eq:expansion}$$ In particular, on $B(x,CR)$ the map $h$ is bi-Lipschitz with scale factor $R^{-1}$ and distortion depending only on $C$. If $d(x,z_0)\ge\varepsilon_*>0$, $R<\varepsilon_*/2$, and $p=hz_0$, then $$d(hy,p)\lesssim R/D_y\quad(y\in Z),\qquad d(hy,p)\asymp R/D_y\quad(d(x,y)\le\varepsilon_*/2), \label{eq:puncture}$$ and $$(v^{-1}\|p)_e\ge k-C(\varepsilon_*). \label{eq:inverse-prefix}$$ The constants in the last two estimates may depend on $\varepsilon_*$.
  - Explanation: Left multiplication by the inverse of a vertex far along a ray distorts boundary distances by an explicit two-sided formula; it magnifies a ball near the ray endpoint and controls the image of the complement through a puncture point.
- **claim-prefixes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-prefix)): Every vertex $v$ lies a uniformly bounded distance from a depth-$\|v\|$ vertex on a based ray. With that ray’s endpoint as $x$, <a href="#eq:expansion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:expansion">[eq:expansion]</a> remains valid for $h=v^{-1}$ and $R=r_{\|v\|}$, with uniform constants. Moreover:* 1. *If $d(\xi,\eta)\le Cr_j$, depth-$j$ vertices on any based rays to $\xi,\eta$ are a bounded distance apart, depending only on $C$.* 2. *If $\|v\|=k$ and $(v\|\xi)_e\ge k-C$, every geodesic from $e$ to $v$ stays a bounded synchronous distance from a ray to $\xi$ through depth $k$.
  - Explanation: Every vertex is tracked by a based ray at the matching depth, so the expansion formula holds for all group elements with uniform constants.
- **claim-packing** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-packing)): There are $Q\ge2$ and $C_P<\infty$ such that an $s$-separated subset of a ball of radius $t$ has at most $C_P(t/s)^Q$ points whenever $0<s\le t\le\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}Z$. In particular, $Z$ is doubling.
  - Explanation: Separated subsets of a ball in the visual boundary obey a polynomial counting bound, so the boundary is doubling.
- **claim-controlled-paths** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-paths)): There is $L\ge1$ such that any two points of $\overline B(x,s)$ join by a path in $B(x,Ls)$. Any two points of a continuum can also be joined by a path in any prescribed open neighborhood of it.
  - Explanation: Points in a small closed ball can be joined by a path staying in a linearly enlarged open ball, and points of a continuum can be joined inside any neighborhood of it.
- **claim-uniformization** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-uniformization)): If $\sup_n M(n)<\infty$, then $(Z,d)$ is quasi-Möbius homeomorphic to the round sphere.
  - Explanation: A uniform bound on the macroscopic combinatorial modulus implies that the visual boundary is quasi-Möbius equivalent to the round sphere, by the cited uniformization criterion.
- **claim-boundary-to-action** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-action)): If $(Z,d)$ is quasi-Möbius homeomorphic to the round sphere, then $G$ admits a proper cocompact isometric action on $\mathbb H^3$ with finite kernel.
  - Explanation: A quasi-Möbius identification of the boundary with the round sphere is straightened by an invariant conformal structure into a proper cocompact isometric action on hyperbolic three-space with finite kernel.
- **claim-rectangle-localization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rectangle-localization)): There are coordinate rectangles $\mathcal Q=[0,1]^2\subset\widehat{\mathcal Q}=[0,1]\times[-1,2]\subset Z$ and a subsequence of the record indices such that the left-to-right crossing modulus of $\mathcal Q$ is at least $cM(N)$, with $c>0$ independent of $N$.
  - Explanation: Unbounded macroscopic modulus localizes to the left-to-right crossing modulus of one coordinate rectangle along record indices.
- **claim-planar-blocker** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-planar-blocker)): In a closed coordinate rectangle:* 1. *every continuum meeting both lateral edges meets every top-to-bottom path;* 2. *every compact set meeting all top-to-bottom paths contains a continuum meeting both lateral edges.
  - Explanation: Planar crossing duality: lateral continua meet every top-to-bottom path, and any compact set blocking all top-to-bottom paths contains a lateral continuum.
- **claim-dual-weights** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-dual-weights)): For each retained $N$ there are nonnegative weights $\rho_q=\rho_q^{(N)}$, $q\in T_N$, admissible for top-to-bottom crossings of $\mathcal Q$, such that $$\sum_{q\in T_N}\rho_q^2\leq \frac{C}{M(N)}. \label{eq:dual-energy}$$
  - Explanation: There are admissible transverse weights whose squared sum is controlled by the inverse of the macroscopic modulus.
- **claim-level-continua** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-level-continua)): The functions $F_N,H_N$ are continuous, with the prescribed band values, and for every path $K\subset\widehat{\mathcal Q}$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_K H_N\leq\mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_K F_N\leq\mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_K U_N^0. \label{eq:flood-osc}$$ For every $0<t<1$, the set $\{H_N=t\}\cap\mathcal Q$ contains a lateral crossing continuum $C_{N,t}$ of diameter at least a fixed $d_1>0$. Every path in $\widehat{\mathcal Q}$ whose endpoint values strictly straddle $t$ meets $C_{N,t}$.
  - Explanation: The regularized functions are continuous with controlled oscillation, and every intermediate level contains a lateral continuum of definite diameter meeting each path straddling the level.
- **claim-local-oscillation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-oscillation)): For fixed compact $K\subset U$ and $J>0$, uniformly for $x\in K$ and sufficiently large $N$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_{\overline B(x,Jr_N)}H_N \leq\sum_{\substack{q\in T_N\\d(q,x)\leq C(J)r_N}}\rho_q. \label{eq:local-osc}$$ Here $C(J)$ depends only on $J$ and the ball path constant. Consequently, for every fixed $g\in G$, compact $K'\subset gU$, and $J\geq2$, $$\mathcal E_N^{(J)}(H_N\circ g^{-1};K') \leq\frac{C(g,K',J)}{M(N)}\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Local oscillation of the regularized function is bounded by nearby dual weights, so the local oscillation energy after any fixed group element tends to zero.
- **claim-annular-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-annular)): For every compact $K\subset U$ there are $R_*>0$ and an integer $m_0$ such that, if $x\in K$, $R=r_l\leq R_*$, and $N-l\geq m_0$, then any two points joined by a path in $B(x,R/2)$ have $H_N$-value difference $\Delta$ satisfying $$\Delta^2\leq C M(N-l) \sum_{\substack{q\in T_N\\R/4\leq d(q,x)\leq4R}}\rho_q^2. \label{eq:annular}$$ The constant is uniform; $N$ is sufficiently large for the fixed interior region.
  - Explanation: Oscillation across a small ball is bounded by the dual energy in a surrounding annulus times the modulus at the shifted scale.
- **claim-scalar-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-scalar-limit)): Along a subsequence of the record indices, $H_N\to u$ locally uniformly on $U$ and $\mu_N\rightharpoonup\mu$, where $u:U\to[0,1]$ is continuous and nonconstant and $\mu$ is finite. The function $u$ is zero and one on the interiors of the respective bands, and is locally Hölder continuous if $\lambda>1$. For every compact $K\subset U$ and sufficiently small $s>0$, uniformly for $x\in K$, $$\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_{\overline B(x,s)}u\bigr)^2 \leq C\mu\bigl(\overline B(x,C_\mu s)\bigr). \label{eq:measure}$$ For each $0<t<1$ there is a continuum $C_t\subset\mathcal Q$ of diameter at least $d_1$, with $u=t$ on $C_t\cap U$, meeting every compact path in $U$ whose endpoint values strictly straddle $t$. The fixed-transform energy bounds of [lem:local-oscillation](#lem:local-oscillation) hold along the retained subsequence.
  - Explanation: Along a subsequence the regularized functions converge locally uniformly to a nonconstant continuous limit and the measures converge weakly to a finite measure bounding squared oscillation; level continua persist in the limit.
- **claim-cross-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cross-energy)): Let $f,w$ be continuous on an open set $V\subset Z$, let $K\subset V$ be compact, and let $I\subset\mathbb R$ be an interval. Suppose that for every $t\in I$ there is a continuum $D_t\subset K$ with $f=t$ on $D_t$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_{D_t}w\geq b_0>0$. For sufficiently large $n$, $$b_0\|I\|\leq \sum_{\substack{q\in T_n\\B(q,2r_n)\cap K\ne\varnothing}} \mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_{\overline B(q,2r_n)}f\, \mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_{\overline B(q,2r_n)}w. \label{eq:cross-energy}$$ Consequently these hypotheses cannot hold for sequences with both local oscillation energies on $K$ tending to zero, if $b_0$ and $\|I\|$ stay bounded below by positive constants.
  - Explanation: A finite-cell inequality: if level continua of one function carry definite variation of a second, the product of local oscillations summed over cells is bounded below, so both energies cannot vanish.
- **claim-stop-continuum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-stop-continuum)): Let $D$ be a continuum, $p\in Z$, and $r>0$. If $x\in D\setminus\overline B(p,r)$ and $D\cap B(p,r)\ne\varnothing$, then $D$ contains a subcontinuum through $x$ meeting the radius-$r$ sphere and avoiding $B(p,r)$.
  - Explanation: A continuum entering an open ball from outside has a subcontinuum through the outside point that reaches the sphere while avoiding the open ball.
- **claim-holder-magnification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-holder-magnification)): Let $f$ be a real-valued, locally Hölder continuous function on an open set $\Omega\subset Z$. Suppose $O$ is open, $\overline O\subset\Omega$, and $O$ contains a compact path on which $f$ is nonconstant. Fix $z_0$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(z_0,O)>0$. There are depths $k_i\to\infty$, centers $x_i\in O$, positive numbers $\sigma_i$, and expansions $h_i=v_i^{-1}$ at $(x_i,k_i)$ such that, after passage to a subsequence, $$p_i=h_i z_0\longrightarrow b, \qquad \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x_i,Z\setminus O)}{r_{k_i}}\longrightarrow\infty.$$ Every compact subset of $Z\setminus\{b\}$ eventually lies in $h_i\Omega$, and $$F_i(z)=\frac{f(h_i^{-1}z)-f(x_i)}{\sigma_i} \label{eq:blowup}$$ converges locally uniformly there to a nonconstant locally Hölder continuous function $F$.
  - Explanation: Normalized magnifications of a locally Hölder function at points of maximal variation converge to a nonconstant locally Hölder function on the sphere minus one puncture.
- **claim-exponential-diagonal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-exponential-diagonal)): After thinning the expansions, there are increasing scalar indices $N_j$ such that $$f_j(z)=\frac{H_{N_j}(h_j^{-1}z)-u(x_j)}{\sigma_j}, \qquad z\in h_j\widehat{\mathcal Q},$$ converges locally uniformly to $W$ on $Z\setminus\{b\}$. For every fixed $g\in G$, compact $K\subset Z\setminus\{gb\}$, and fixed $J\ge2$, $$\mathcal E_{N_j}^{(J)}(f_j\circ g^{-1};K)\longrightarrow0. \label{eq:exponential-translated-energy}$$ All indicated enlarged balls eventually lie in the functions’ domains.* *If a compact path $P\subset Z\setminus\{b\}$ has endpoint values of $W$ strictly straddling a compact positive-length interval $I$, then, for all large $j$ and every $t\in I$, there is a continuum $D_{j,t}\subset h_j\widehat{\mathcal Q}$ with $$f_j=t\text{ on }D_{j,t},\qquad D_{j,t}\cap P\ne\varnothing, \qquad \sup_{t\in I}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(b,D_{j,t})\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: A diagonal choice of approximation indices gives approximants converging to the magnified limit, with vanishing energies after fixed group elements and level continua escaping toward the puncture.
- **claim-exponential-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-exponential-comparison)): If $c=gb\ne b$ and $V=W\circ g^{-1}$, then $$V(x)=V(b)\qquad \bigl(x\in\mathcal N(W)\setminus\{c\}\bigr). \label{eq:transform-level}$$ More generally, for $W_s=W\circ s^{-1}$ and $p_s=sb$, if $p_s\ne p_t$, $$W_t(x)=W_t(p_s)\qquad \bigl(x\in\mathcal N(W_s)\setminus\{p_t\}\bigr). \label{eq:two-transform-level}$$
  - Explanation: A transform with a different puncture is constant on the variation set of the original magnified function away from that puncture.
- **claim-stabilizer-directions** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-stabilizer)): For every $b\in Z$, the vertices of $E=\mathop{\mathrm{Stab}}_G(b)$ have at most two accumulation directions.
  - Explanation: The stabilizer of a boundary point has linear growth and at most two accumulation directions.
- **claim-no-exponential-records** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-no-exponential-records)): The contradiction setup cannot have $\lambda>1$.
  - Explanation: A second magnification yields two transforms that would have to be constant on each other's variation sets in two disks with disjoint ranges, excluding exponential record growth.
- **claim-bad-node-children** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-bad-node-children)): For sufficiently large $D$, every node that is not good has at least $c_Ha^D$ mutually $Hr$-separated candidates with $t_y\geq1/8$, where $c_H>0$ is independent of the node and $D$.
  - Explanation: A node failing the two-separated-variations condition has many separated candidate children each carrying a definite fraction of the interval.
- **claim-separated-capacity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-separated-capacity)): For sufficiently large $D$, uniformly over all nodes, there is an $Hr$-separated subset $Y'\subset Y$ such that $$\sum_{y\in Y'}\min\{a^{-\gamma D}t_y,a^{\tau D}t_y^2\}\geq1. \label{eq:capacity}$$
  - Explanation: Modulus at an intermediate scale shows that the combined linear and quadratic capacities of a separated set of candidates reach the required total.
- **claim-good-levels** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-good-levels)): Infinitely many generations have total probability at least $1/2$ on good nodes.
  - Explanation: Conservation of probability and the finite level energy force substantial mass on good nodes at infinitely many generations.
- **claim-probability-flow** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-probability-flow)): The construction gives a fixed $D$ and a tree with generation-$n$ centers $Hr_{k_0+Dn}$-separated and descendant displacement <a href="#eq:descendant-displacement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:descendant-displacement">[eq:descendant-displacement]</a>. Its nonempty value intervals are nested, its probabilities satisfy <a href="#eq:flow" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:flow">[eq:flow]</a> and <a href="#eq:conditional-flow" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:conditional-flow">[eq:conditional-flow]</a>, and infinitely many levels have good mass at least $1/2$. Every node has a fixed expansion. Each good node has a pattern from a finite list of ordered target-ball pairs. For that expansion, each target ball contains a path with endpoint variation at least $\kappa\Delta_i$, their whole-ball range intervals have distance at least $\Delta_i/4$, and the balls lie in the images of the respective outer balls of radius $c_0\theta r_{k_0+Dn}$.
  - Explanation: The tree construction yields separated centers, nested intervals, probability bounds, and good nodes carrying separated target patterns from a finite list.
- **claim-no-subexponential-records** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-no-subexponential-records)): The moduli $M(n)$ cannot be unbounded while satisfying $$\limsup_{n\to\infty}M(n)^{1/n}\leq1.$$
  - Explanation: Comparing same-generation expansions among far punctures produces more measure than overlap allows, excluding unbounded moduli with subexponential growth.
- **claim-puncture-multiplicity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-puncture-multiplicity)): For every fixed $C>0$, the balls $\{\overline B(p_i,CR):i\text{ at generation }n\}$ have uniformly bounded multiplicity.
  - Explanation: Puncture balls of fixed relative radius at one generation overlap only boundedly many times.
- **claim-near-puncture-probability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-near-puncture-probability)): For every generation-$n$ node $i$, $$\sum_{\substack{j\text{ at generation }n\\d(p_j,p_i)\le a^{-l}}}P_j \le Ca^{-\gamma Dm}. \label{eq:near-puncture-probability}$$
  - Explanation: The total probability of nodes whose punctures lie near a given puncture decays at an explicit exponential rate.
- **claim-pair-alternative** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pair-alternative)): Every far pair at a sufficiently large generation has an endpoint $i$ satisfying $$\mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_{h_i^{-1}E_j}u\ge\frac{\kappa}{16}\Delta_i. \label{eq:pair-alternative}$$
  - Explanation: For every far pair one endpoint spends a definite amount of oscillation near the other's puncture.
- **claim-torsion-free-manifold** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-torsion-free)): If $G$ is a torsion-free hyperbolic group and $\partial G$ is homeomorphic to $\mathbb S^2$, then $G$ is the fundamental group of a closed hyperbolic three-manifold.
  - Explanation: For torsion-free groups the action is free, so the quotient is a closed hyperbolic three-manifold.
- **claim-virtual-structure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-virtual-structure)): Let $G$ be a torsion-free hyperbolic group with $\partial G$ homeomorphic to $\mathbb S^2$. Some finite-index subgroup of $G$ is isomorphic to $\pi_1(\Sigma)\rtimes\mathbb Z$ for a closed connected orientable surface $\Sigma$. In particular, $G$ has positive virtual first Betti number. Moreover, $G$ is virtually compact special and residually finite.
  - Explanation: A torsion-free group with two-sphere boundary virtually fibers, has positive virtual first Betti number, and is virtually compact special and residually finite.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-oscillation",
        "exact_quote": "For fixed compact $K\\subset U$ and $J>0$, uniformly for $x\\in K$ and sufficiently large $N$, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{osc}}_{\\overline B(x,Jr_N)}H_N\n \\leq\\sum_{\\substack{q\\in T_N\\\\d(q,x)\\leq C(J)r_N}}\\rho_q.\n \\label{eq:local-osc}$$ Here $C(J)$ depends only on $J$ and the ball path constant. Consequently, for every fixed $g\\in G$, compact $K'\\subset gU$, and $J\\geq2$, $$\\mathcal E_N^{(J)}(H_N\\circ g^{-1};K')\n       \\leq\\frac{C(g,K',J)}{M(N)}\\longrightarrow0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-246.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-oscillation",
          "exact_quote": "For fixed compact $K\\subset U$ and $J>0$, uniformly for $x\\in K$ and sufficiently large $N$, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{osc}}_{\\overline B(x,Jr_N)}H_N\n \\leq\\sum_{\\substack{q\\in T_N\\\\d(q,x)\\leq C(J)r_N}}\\rho_q.\n \\label{eq:local-osc}$$ Here $C(J)$ depends only on $J$ and the ball path constant. Consequently, for every fixed $g\\in G$, compact $K'\\subset gU$, and $J\\geq2$, $$\\mathcal E_N^{(J)}(H_N\\circ g^{-1};K')\n       \\leq\\frac{C(g,K',J)}{M(N)}\\longrightarrow0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-246.1"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-annular",
        "exact_quote": "For every compact $K\\subset U$ there are $R_*>0$ and an integer $m_0$ such that, if $x\\in K$, $R=r_l\\leq R_*$, and $N-l\\geq m_0$, then any two points joined by a path in $B(x,R/2)$ have $H_N$-value difference $\\Delta$ satisfying $$\\Delta^2\\leq C M(N-l)\n       \\sum_{\\substack{q\\in T_N\\\\R/4\\leq d(q,x)\\leq4R}}\\rho_q^2.\n \\label{eq:annular}$$ The constant is uniform; $N$ is sufficiently large for the fixed interior region.",
        "source_id": "PM-246.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-246.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-scalar-limit",
        "exact_quote": "Along a subsequence of the record indices, $H_N\\to u$ locally uniformly on $U$ and $\\mu_N\\rightharpoonup\\mu$, where $u:U\\to[0,1]$ is continuous and nonconstant and $\\mu$ is finite. The function $u$ is zero and one on the interiors of the respective bands, and is locally Hölder continuous if $\\lambda>1$. For every compact $K\\subset U$ and sufficiently small $s>0$, uniformly for $x\\in K$, $$\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{osc}}_{\\overline B(x,s)}u\\bigr)^2\n       \\leq C\\mu\\bigl(\\overline B(x,C_\\mu s)\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:measure}$$ For each $0<t<1$ there is a continuum $C_t\\subset\\mathcal Q$ of diameter at least $d_1$, with $u=t$ on $C_t\\cap U$, meeting every compact path in $U$ whose endpoint values strictly straddle $t$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cross-energy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f,w$ be continuous on an open set $V\\subset Z$, let $K\\subset V$ be compact, and let $I\\subset\\mathbb R$ be an interval. Suppose that for every $t\\in I$ there is a continuum $D_t\\subset K$ with $f=t$ on $D_t$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{osc}}_{D_t}w\\geq b_0>0$. For sufficiently large $n$, $$b_0|I|\\leq\n \\sum_{\\substack{q\\in T_n\\\\B(q,2r_n)\\cap K\\ne\\varnothing}}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{osc}}_{\\overline B(q,2r_n)}f\\,\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{osc}}_{\\overline B(q,2r_n)}w.\n \\label{eq:cross-energy}$$ Consequently these hypotheses cannot hold for sequences with both local oscillation energies on $K$ tending to zero, if $b_0$ and $|I|$ stay bounded below by positive constants.",
        "source_id": "PM-246.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cross-energy",
          "exact_quote": "Let $f,w$ be continuous on an open set $V\\subset Z$, let $K\\subset V$ be compact, and let $I\\subset\\mathbb R$ be an interval. For sufficiently large $n$, $$b_0|I|\\leq\n \\sum_{\\substack{q\\in T_n\\\\B(q,2r_n)\\cap K\\ne\\varnothing}}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{osc}}_{\\overline B(q,2r_n)}f\\,\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{osc}}_{\\overline B(q,2r_n)}w.\n \\label{eq:cross-energy}$$ Consequently these hypotheses cannot hold for sequences with both local oscillation energies on $K$ tending to zero, if $b_0$ and $|I|$ stay bounded below by positive constants.",
          "source_id": "PM-246.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $f,w$ be continuous on an open set $V\\subset Z$, let $K\\subset V$ be compact, and let $I\\subset\\mathbb R$ be an interval.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-holder-magnification",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f$ be a real-valued, locally Hölder continuous function on an open set $\\Omega\\subset Z$. Suppose $O$ is open, $\\overline O\\subset\\Omega$, and $O$ contains a compact path on which $f$ is nonconstant. Fix $z_0$ with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(z_0,O)>0$. There are depths $k_i\\to\\infty$, centers $x_i\\in O$, positive numbers $\\sigma_i$, and expansions $h_i=v_i^{-1}$ at $(x_i,k_i)$ such that, after passage to a subsequence, $$p_i=h_i z_0\\longrightarrow b,\n \\qquad\n \\frac{\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x_i,Z\\setminus O)}{r_{k_i}}\\longrightarrow\\infty.$$ Every compact subset of $Z\\setminus\\{b\\}$ eventually lies in $h_i\\Omega$, and $$F_i(z)=\\frac{f(h_i^{-1}z)-f(x_i)}{\\sigma_i}\n \\label{eq:blowup}$$ converges locally uniformly there to a nonconstant locally Hölder continuous function $F$.",
        "source_id": "PM-246.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-holder-magnification",

There are depths $k_i\\to\\infty$, centers $x_i\\in O$, positive numbers $\\sigma_i$, and expansions $h_i=v_i^{-1}$ at $(x_i,k_i)$ such that, after passage to a subsequence, $$p_i=h_i z_0\\longrightarrow b,\n \\qquad\n \\frac{\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x_i,Z\\setminus O)}{r_{k_i}}\\longrightarrow\\infty.$$ Every compact subset of $Z\\setminus\\{b\\}$ eventually lies in $h_i\\Omega$, and $$F_i(z)=\\frac{f(h_i^{-1}z)-f(x_i)}{\\sigma_i}\n \\label{eq:blowup}$$ converges locally uniformly there to a nonconstant locally Hölder continuous function $F$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **hyperbolic group** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Here a group is *hyperbolic* if a Cayley graph for a finite generating set has uniformly thin geodesic triangles; its boundary consists of asymptotic classes of geodesic rays, with the usual visual topology.
- **geometric action** ([source](#sec:introduction)): An isometric action is *geometric* if it is proper and cocompact.
- **Cayley graph and word length** ([source](#eq:visual)): Let $\Gamma$ be the Cayley graph of $G$ for a finite symmetric generating set, with unit edge lengths.
- **Gromov product** ([source](#eq:visual)): The Gromov product is $$(y\|z)_o=\tfrac12\bigl(d_\Gamma(o,y)+d_\Gamma(o,z)-d_\Gamma(y,z)\bigr).$$
- **visual metric** ([source](#eq:visual)): A visual metric $d$ on $Z=\partial G$ satisfies $$C_v^{-1}a^{-(y\|z)_e}\le d(y,z)\le C_va^{-(y\|z)_e}, \qquad y\ne z, \label{eq:visual}$$ for some $a>1$.
- **scales r_k** ([source](#eq:visual)): Set $r_k=a^{-k}$ for integers $k\ge0$.
- **separated net and cells** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Choose a maximal $r_n$-separated set $T_n\subset Z$ and its cells $B(q,2r_n)$, $q\in T_n$.
- **combinatorial 2-modulus** ([source](#pm-h-9)): For a path family $\mathcal F$, define $$\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}_2(\mathcal F,T_n)=\min_{\omega\ge0} \left\{\sum_{q\in T_n}\omega(q)^2: \sum_{B(q,2r_n)\cap\pi\ne\varnothing}\omega(q)\ge1 \text{ for every }\pi\in\mathcal F\right\}.$$
- **macroscopic modulus M(n)** ([source](#eq:macro-modulus)): $$\mathcal F_0=\{\pi:\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}\pi\ge d_0\},\qquad M(n)=\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}_2(\mathcal F_0,T_n).
- **approximate self-similarity** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Approximate self-similarity means that each open ball, with distances divided by its radius, is uniformly bi-Lipschitz to an open subset of the space.
- **continuum** ([source](#pm-h-5)): A continuum is a nonempty compact connected set; it need not be path connected.
- **oscillation** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For real $f$, write $\mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_A f=\sup_A f-\inf_A f$.
- **domain U** ([source](#pm-h-13)): Put $U=\operatorname{int}_Z\widehat{\mathcal Q}$.
- **non-local-constancy set** ([source](#pm-h-19)): Let $\mathcal N(F)$ be the points of a continuous function’s domain at which it is not locally constant.
- **pattern of a good node** ([source](#pm-h-25)): The ordered pair $(\mathcal B^-,\mathcal B^+)$ is the node’s *pattern*.
- **Ahlfors regular conformal dimension** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A related route uses *Ahlfors regular conformal dimension*: the infimum of the Hausdorff dimensions of Ahlfors regular metrics quasisymmetric to the visual metric.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-visual-base | base a of the visual metric | > | $1$ | Fixed once for the whole paper |
| q-packing-exponent | packing exponent Q | ≥ | $2$ | Fixed constant |
| q-packing-count | size of an s-separated subset of a ball of radius t | ≤ | $C_P(t/s)^Q$ | All admissible s and t |
| q-path-factor | path enlargement factor L | ≥ | $1$ | Uniform in x and s |
| q-dual-energy | squared sum of dual weights | ≤ | $\frac{C}{M(N)}$ | Retained record indices |
| q-record-comparison | modulus at an earlier scale | ≤ | $\lambda^{-l}M(N)$ | Range of l up to N |
| q-level-diameter | diameter of a level continuum | ≥ | $d_1$ | Fixed positive d_1 |
| q-holder-exponent | local Hölder exponent of the scalar limit | = | $\log\lambda/(2\log a)$ | Case lambda greater than one |
| q-measure-oscillation | squared oscillation on a small ball | ≤ | $C\mu\bigl(\overline B(x,C_\mu s)\bigr)$ | Sufficiently small s, uniform in x in K |
| q-stabilizer-growth | number of stabilizer elements of word length at most t | ≤ | $C'(t+1)$ | All t |
| q-subexponential-growth | growth rate of M(j) | ≤ | $1$ | Limit superior as j tends to infinity |
| q-flow-b | quadratic exponent b_* | = | $\frac12$ | Fixed for all nodes |
| q-flow-tau | exponent tau | = | $\frac1{20}$ | Fixed for all nodes |
| q-flow-beta | exponent beta | = | $\frac{\tau}{4(Q+1)}$ | Fixed for all nodes |
| q-capacity-total | separated capacity sum | ≥ | $1$ | Some Hr-separated subset |
| q-descendant-displacement | displacement of descendants from a parent | ≤ | $2(B_0+1)R$ | Parent of scale R |
| q-level-energy | sum of squared interval lengths at one level | ≤ | $E_0$ | E_0 is a constant times the total mass of the limit measure |
| q-branch-probability | probability of a node | ≤ | $a^{-\gamma Dn}\frac{\Delta_i}{\Delta_0}$ | All nodes |
| q-near-puncture | probability near a given puncture | ≤ | $Ca^{-\gamma Dm}$ | Punctures within the stated distance |
| q-cost-lower | measure of a cost ball | ≥ | $c\kappa^2\Delta_i^2$ | Qualifying endpoints |
| q-final-contradiction | exponential in the generation | ≤ | $Ck^2$ | Large generations; contradicts k growing linearly |
| q-orientation-index | index of the orientation subgroup | ≤ | $2$ | Torsion-free case |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-expansion** [claim](#stmt-lem-expansion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-expansion): Base change and thin triangles for Gromov products, then exponentiation -> two-sided distortion formula for left multiplication by a vertex inverse
- **step-prefixes** [claim](#stmt-lem-prefix) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prefix): Ideal triangle through a translated bi-infinite geodesic -> every vertex is near a ray vertex of matching depth
- **step-packing** [claim](#stmt-lem-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-packing): Counting ray continuations at depth beyond the ball scale -> polynomial packing bound and doubling
- **step-paths** [claim](#stmt-lem-paths) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-paths): Expand, join in the sphere away from the puncture, pull back -> controlled paths in enlarged balls and in neighborhoods of continua
- **step-uniformization** [claim](#stmt-thm-uniformization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-uniformization): Verify the hypotheses of the cited uniformization criterion -> quasi-Möbius equivalence with the round sphere
- **step-action** [claim](#stmt-prop-action) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-action): Invariant conformal structure by circumcenters, measurable Riemann mapping, then properness on triples -> proper cocompact isometric action with finite kernel
- **step-localization** [claim](#stmt-lem-rectangle-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rectangle-localization): Finite cover by coordinate boxes and pigeonhole over crossing families -> one rectangle with comparable crossing modulus
- **step-planar-blocker** [claim](#stmt-lem-planar-blocker) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-planar-blocker): Jordan curve argument and a grid interface parity argument -> crossing duality between lateral continua and top-to-bottom paths
- **step-dual-weights** [claim](#stmt-lem-dual-weights) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dual-weights): Least-norm point in a convex hull of incidence vectors -> admissible dual weights with small energy
- **step-level-continua** [claim](#stmt-lem-level-continua) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-level-continua): Path-envelope regularization and connection of strict sides to the bands -> continuous functions with separating level continua
- **step-local-oscillation** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-oscillation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-oscillation): Join points by controlled paths, bound by nearby weights, sum with packing -> local oscillation bound and vanishing energy after fixed transformations
- **step-annular** [claim](#stmt-lem-annular) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-annular): Expand the annulus to macroscopic size and test with macroscopic weights -> annular oscillation estimate
- **step-scalar-limit** [claim](#stmt-prop-scalar-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-scalar-limit): Equicontinuity from annular and record estimates, then compactness extraction -> nonconstant limit function and finite limit measure
- **step-cross-energy** [claim](#stmt-lem-cross-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cross-energy): Cover value intervals by cell range intervals, integrate over levels, Cauchy–Schwarz -> lower bound on product of local oscillations
- **step-stop-continuum** [claim](#stmt-lem-stop-continuum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stop-continuum): Components in compact metric spaces and clopen neighborhoods -> subcontinuum reaching the sphere outside the ball
- **step-holder-magnification** [claim](#stmt-lem-holder-magnification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-holder-magnification): Choose points of near-maximal weighted oscillation at tail-maximal scales, expand, extract a limit -> nonconstant locally Hölder function on the punctured sphere
- **step-exp-diagonal** [claim](#stmt-lem-exponential-diagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exponential-diagonal): Diagonal choice of approximation indices with the expansion held fixed -> approximants with vanishing energy and escaping level continua
- **step-exp-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-exponential-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exponential-comparison): Contradiction via stopped subcontinua and the cross-energy inequality -> transforms with different punctures are constant on variation sets
- **step-stabilizer** [claim](#stmt-lem-stabilizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stabilizer): Linear growth of the stabilizer and loxodromic endpoint analysis -> at most two accumulation directions
- **step-no-exp** [claim](#stmt-prop-no-exponential-records) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-no-exponential-records): Second magnification with distinct punctures contradicting disjoint ranges -> exponential records are impossible
- **step-bad-node** [claim](#stmt-lem-bad-node-children) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bad-node-children): Level continua cross radial spheres; cover the interval by inner ranges -> many separated large children at a node that is not good
- **step-capacity** [claim](#stmt-lem-separated-capacity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-separated-capacity): Graph coloring, marked-measure removal and detection by modulus at an intermediate scale -> separated set with capacity at least the required total
- **step-flow** [claim](#stmt-prop-probability-flow) / [proof](#eq:flow): Recursive tree construction with transition probabilities and telescoping bounds -> tree with flow bounds and level energy bound
- **step-good-levels** [claim](#stmt-lem-good-levels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-good-levels): Jensen's inequality on the flow bound and conservation of probability -> infinitely many generations with good mass at least one half
- **step-puncture-multiplicity** [claim](#stmt-lem-puncture-multiplicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-puncture-multiplicity): Bounded centers per vertex and bounded inverse vertices -> bounded multiplicity of puncture balls
- **step-near-puncture** [claim](#stmt-lem-near-puncture-probability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-near-puncture-probability): Split geodesic words into prefix and suffix and count descendants of each ancestor -> exponential decay of probability near a puncture
- **step-pair-alternative** [claim](#stmt-lem-pair-alternative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pair-alternative): Contradiction through stopped continua and the cross-energy inequality for a fixed pair -> one endpoint of each far pair has definite oscillation near the other puncture
- **step-no-subexp** [claim](#stmt-prop-no-subexponential-records) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pair-alternative-2): Charge measure to cost balls; compare upper multiplicity bound with lower pair-count bound -> subexponential unbounded moduli are impossible
- **step-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pair-alternative-3): Contradiction: unbounded modulus gives exponential or subexponential records, both excluded -> proper cocompact isometric action with finite kernel
- **step-torsion-free** [claim](#stmt-cor-torsion-free) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-torsion-free): Finite stabilizers are trivial in a torsion-free group, so the action is free -> closed hyperbolic three-manifold with the given fundamental group
- **step-virtual** [claim](#stmt-cor-virtual-structure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-virtual-structure): Orientation cover, then cited virtual fibering, cubulation, virtual specialness and separability theorems -> virtual fibering, positive virtual Betti number, virtual compact specialness, residual finiteness

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-main-geometric-action**: For a hyperbolic group whose boundary is homeomorphic to the two-sphere, there is a homomorphism into the isometry group of hyperbolic three-space whose action is proper and cocompact, with finite kernel.
- **inv-bounded-modulus-uniformization**: If the macroscopic combinatorial moduli are uniformly bounded, the visual boundary is quasi-Möbius homeomorphic to the round sphere.
- **inv-torsion-free-manifold**: A torsion-free hyperbolic group with two-sphere boundary is the fundamental group of a closed hyperbolic three-manifold.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-non-spherical-boundary**: Applying the geometric-action conclusion to a hyperbolic group whose boundary is not a two-sphere. -> The theorem gives no conclusion; the argument uses the two-sphere topology of the boundary throughout.
- **anti-trivial-kernel-assumption**: Assuming the action is faithful or free for a group with torsion. -> Only finiteness of the kernel is asserted; the quotient need not be a manifold when torsion is present.
- **anti-nonconstructive-limits**: Reading the weak-limit contradiction argument as a numerical procedure for computing the action or the moduli. -> The proof proceeds by contradiction through subsequences, so it provides no rates, finite procedure or certified output.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is existential: boundedness of the moduli is proved by contradiction, using subsequential limits of functions and measures, so no finite constructive procedure with checkable inputs and outputs is defined.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-boundary-sphere** (hypothesis): The main theorem requires the group to be hyperbolic with boundary homeomorphic to the two-sphere. Without that boundary topology no conclusion is drawn. Blocked use: ['Applying the geometric action on hyperbolic three-space to hyperbolic groups whose boundary is not a two-sphere']
- **limit-finite-kernel** (edge_case): The action given by the main theorem has only a finite kernel. It is not asserted to be faithful or free for groups with torsion. Blocked use: ['Assuming a faithful action or a manifold quotient for a group with torsion']
- **limit-torsion-free-manifold** (hypothesis): The closed hyperbolic three-manifold conclusion is proved only when the group is torsion-free, and the manifold may be nonorientable. Blocked use: ['Claiming an orientable manifold quotient', 'Applying the manifold statement to groups with torsion']
- **limit-uniformization-conditional** (hypothesis): The uniformization step is conditional on a uniform bound of the macroscopic moduli over all scales. Any use of it outside that bound is unsupported. Blocked use: ['Concluding quasi-Möbius equivalence with the round sphere from a bound at finitely many scales']
- **limit-nonconstructive-proof** (nonconstructive): Boundedness of the moduli is shown by contradiction, with limits taken along subsequences. No rate, procedure or algorithm is supplied. Blocked use: ['Using the scalar limit or the measure as a numerical algorithm', 'Extracting an explicit uniformizing map or explicit action']
- **limit-growth-case-split** (formal_scope): The contradiction splits into an exponential case and a subexponential case. Each exclusion is proved only under its own growth hypothesis, and both are needed together. Blocked use: ['Using either proposition alone to rule out unbounded moduli']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 246 (`lean/docs/246.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A Modulus Proof of Cannon’s Conjecture](../../preprints/A-Modulus-Proof-of-Cannons-Conjecture-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Cannon's conjecture asks whether a hyperbolic group with boundary homeomorphic to the two-sphere acts geometrically on hyperbolic three-space. The formalization proves that such a group admits an isometric action on $\mathbb H^3$ that is proper and cocompact and has finite kernel. Hyperbolicity is expressed through uniformly thin geodesic triangles in a Cayley graph, and the boundary hypothesis is a homeomorphism with $S^2$.

Comparator source `CannonGeometricAction.lean` (3896 bytes, sha256 `210b5bfe96ace15ecacccd7285095d2e2a5679c35f182257c02a9ce276190b54`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section
namespace CannonRelease

universe u

structure CayleyData (G : Type u) [Group G] where
  generators : Finset G
  symmetric : ∀ x ∈ generators, x⁻¹ ∈ generators
  connected : (SimpleGraph.mulCayley (generators : Set G)).Connected

def CayleyData.graph {G : Type u} [Group G] (D : CayleyData G) : SimpleGraph G :=
  SimpleGraph.mulCayley (D.generators : Set G)

/-- A uniform thinness constant for geodesic triangles in the Cayley graph. -/
def CayleyData.ThinTriangles {G : Type u} [Group G] (D : CayleyData G) : Prop :=
  ∃ δ : ℕ, ∀ x y z : G,
    ∀ p : D.graph.Walk x y, ∀ q : D.graph.Walk y z, ∀ r : D.graph.Walk z x,
      p.length = D.graph.dist x y → q.length = D.graph.dist y z →
      r.length = D.graph.dist z x →
      ∀ a ∈ p.support, ∃ b ∈ q.support ++ r.support, D.graph.dist a b ≤ δ

/-- Based geodesic rays, parametrized at integer times. -/
def BasedRay {G : Type u} [Group G] (D : CayleyData G) :=
  {r : ℕ → G // r 0 = 1 ∧ ∀ i j, D.graph.dist (r i) (r j) = Nat.dist i j}

instance {G : Type u} [Group G] [TopologicalSpace G] (D : CayleyData G) :
    TopologicalSpace (BasedRay D) := inferInstanceAs (TopologicalSpace {r : ℕ → G //
      r 0 = 1 ∧ ∀ i j, D.graph.dist (r i) (r j) = Nat.dist i j})

/-- Finite synchronous distance, equivalent to asymptoticity for based rays. -/
def raySetoid {G : Type u} [Group G] (D : CayleyData G) : Setoid (BasedRay D) where
  r r s := ∃ C : ℕ, ∀ n, D.graph.dist (r.val n) (s.val n) ≤ C
  iseqv := by
    constructor
    · intro r
      exact ⟨0, fun n => by simp⟩
    · rintro r s ⟨C, hC⟩
      exact ⟨C, fun n => by rw [SimpleGraph.dist_comm]; exact hC n⟩
    · rintro r s t ⟨C, hC⟩ ⟨E, hE⟩
      refine ⟨C + E, fun n => ?_⟩
      exact le_trans ((D.connected (r.val n) (s.val n)).dist_triangle_left (t.val n))
        (Nat.add_le_add (hC n) (hE n))

def Boundary {G : Type u} [Group G] (D : CayleyData G) := Quotient (raySetoid D)

instance {G : Type u} [Group G] [TopologicalSpace G] (D : CayleyData G) :
    TopologicalSpace (Boundary D) :=
  inferInstanceAs (TopologicalSpace (Quotient (raySetoid D)))

abbrev SphereTwo := {x : EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin 3) // x ∈ Metric.sphere 0 1}

/-- The upper-half-space model of hyperbolic three-space. -/
def HyperbolicThree := {x : Fin 3 → ℝ // 0 < x 2}

instance : TopologicalSpace HyperbolicThree :=
  inferInstanceAs (TopologicalSpace {x : Fin 3 → ℝ // 0 < x 2})

def hyperbolicDist (x y : HyperbolicThree) : ℝ :=
  Real.arcosh (1 + (∑ i : Fin 3, (x.val i - y.val i) ^ 2) / (2 * x.val 2 * y.val 2))

/-- The full isometry group, including orientation-reversing isometries. -/
def hyperbolicIsometries : Subgroup (HyperbolicThree ≃ₜ HyperbolicThree) where
  carrier := {f | ∀ x y, hyperbolicDist (f x) (f y) = hyperbolicDist x y}
  one_mem' := by
    change ∀ x y, hyperbolicDist x y = hyperbolicDist x y
    intros
    rfl
  mul_mem' := by
    intro f g hf hg x y
    exact (hf (g x) (g y)).trans (hg x y)
  inv_mem' := by
    intro f hf x y
    simpa using (hf (f.symm x) (f.symm y)).symm

abbrev H3Isom := ↥hyperbolicIsometries

def ProperAction {G : Type u} [Group G] (ρ : G →* H3Isom) : Prop :=
  ∀ K : Set HyperbolicThree, IsCompact K →
    {g : G | ∃ x ∈ K, (ρ g).val x ∈ K}.Finite

def CocompactAction {G : Type u} [Group G] (ρ : G →* H3Isom) : Prop :=
  ∃ K : Set HyperbolicThree, IsCompact K ∧
    ∀ x : HyperbolicThree, ∃ g : G, ∃ y ∈ K, (ρ g).val y = x

theorem cannon (G : Type u) [Group G] [TopologicalSpace G] [DiscreteTopology G]
    (D : CayleyData G) (hthin : D.ThinTriangles)
    (hsphere : Nonempty (Boundary D ≃ₜ SphereTwo)) :
    ∃ ρ : G →* H3Isom, ProperAction ρ ∧ CocompactAction ρ ∧
      (ρ.ker : Set G).Finite := by
  sorry

end CannonRelease
end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that every hyperbolic group whose boundary is homeomorphic to the two-sphere admits a proper cocompact isometric action on hyperbolic three-space with finite kernel. This resolves Cannon's conjecture positively.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-thm-main](#stmt-thm-main) | theorem | `thm:main` | 1 | Let $G$ be a hyperbolic group with $\partial G$ homeomorphic to $\mathbb S^2$. |
| [stmt-lem-expansion](#stmt-lem-expansion) | lemma | `lem:expansion` | 1 | Let $v$ be the depth-$k$ vertex on a based ray to $x\in Z$, put $h=v^{-1}$ and $R=r_k$, and set $D_y=\max\{R,d(x,y)\}$. |
